ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The aim of this draft Law on amending the Budget Law for 2016 is to allow 70% of payments in the current year. advance payments for direct payments to agricultural producers for 2016 under the common agricultural policy of the European Union amounting to 10.3 billion. This is allowed by the European Commission regulation of 8 September, even in its letter of 9 September, the European Commission encourages European countries to forward advance payments to farmers due to the difficult situation on the agricultural markets of the European Union. The payment of these advances is also intended to mitigate the negative effects of the crisis on agricultural markets caused by the fall in agricultural fetal prices, also linked to the Russian embargo. The proposed amendment is to reduce the planned revenue of the European budget under the common agricultural policy by PLN 8.9 billion in 2016 and increase the deficit of the European budget by the same identical amount. This will allow for the implementation of the aforementioned. Target. Therefore, the 2016 Budget Act proposes the following changes: reducing the projected revenue of the European budget under the common agricultural policy to 17.6 billion and thus reducing the revenue of the European budget to 53,4 billion. As a result, the budget deficit for Europe will amount to 18.2 billion. And the second element - a change in the deficit of the European budget implies a change in the revenue and expenditure of the state budget. Therefore, the total amount of planned revenue of the State budget of PLN 338 billion and the total amount of planned expenditure of the State budget of PLN 265.1 billion are fixed. The planned balance of revenue and expenditure of the state budget will amount to almost PLN 73 billion. Given that the proposed change concerns only the deficit of the budget of European funds, it has no impact on the deficit of the public finance sector according to ESA 2010, while the result of the proposed change may be an increase in public debt by a maximum of PLN 9 billion. I've been round here before I said: 8.9 billion PLN. In accordance with EU rules, the reimbursement of expenditure incurred by the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund will take place in January 2017 and will contribute to the budget of European funds under the common agricultural policy. So this is a kind of transfer of funds - an earlier allocation for payments to farmers and in 3 months from now practically compensation for the common agricultural policy back to the state budget. In view of the need to achieve the purpose of the Act as soon as possible and the fact that advances can be paid from 17 October to 30 November - during this period we can pay - I propose that the Act enter into force on the day following the date of publication. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u18	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Prime Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the budget law for 2016 in print No. 916. The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes this proposal. This conclusion is due to the urgent need to exploit the opportunity created by the European Commission Implementing Regulation of 8 September 2016 in view of the difficult situation on agricultural markets, according to which Member States are able to increase to 70% the amount of advances allowed for direct payments. The adoption of the bill will allow the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development to pay a 70% advance on direct payments to Polish agricultural producers for 2016 under the common agricultural policy, which will alleviate the negative effects of the crisis on agricultural markets due to falling agricultural prices. The amount of PLN 10 265 million is necessary to secure these payments between 17 October and 30 November 2016. In view of the objective reasons for non-fulfilment of the full revenue of the European budget under the common agricultural policy, it is necessary to amend the 2016 budget law in this respect - this applies to the missing amount of PLN 8971 million - by reducing the amount of revenue of the European budget in 2016 under the heading: the Common Agricultural Policy. The other changes are the implications of the above amendment. It should also be underlined that the proposed change does not affect the public finance sector deficit according to ESA 2010. Since advances can be paid from 17 October, we fully support the bill and its entry into force on the day following the date of publication. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u20	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present a position towards the Government's draft law amending the Budget Act for 2016, Parliamentary Printing No. 916. The revision of the budget law is due to the possibility of using the revision of the Union Regulation, which entered into force on 8 September 2016. According to the previous Union Regulation, Member States could pay advance payments of up to 50% in the case of direct payments before 1 December but not before 16 October. In view of the difficult situation on agricultural markets on 8 September, the European Commission decided to waive this principle and this year the countries of the European Union have the opportunity to increase to 70% the amount of advances allowed for direct payments. In the explanatory memorandum of the project, the Minister explains that this change will enable the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development to pay 70% in the current year. advances for direct payments to agricultural producers for 2016 under the common agricultural policy. The change involves among others. to increase the budget deficit of European funds by PLN 8971 million, which will allow implementation of the above mentioned. Target. Originally, the revenue and expenditure of the budget of European funds under the common agricultural policy were planned in the 2016 budget law of 26 billion. In his explanatory memorandum, the Minister states that, given that the proposed change concerns only the deficit of European funds, it has no impact on the deficit of the public finance sector. On the other hand, the proposed change will result in a maximum increase in public debt of 9 billion. However, the final amount of the debt increase will depend on the implementation of the financing process for loan needs and the resulting level of resources collected in the state budget accounts at the end of 2016. It was indicated that the payment was 70% from 17 October to 30 November 2016. Advances require almost 10 billion European funds. It is also added that due to the incomplete implementation of the revenue of the European budget under the common agricultural policy in the 2016 budget act, it is necessary to implement these expenditures under the deficit of the European budget, which is increased by 8 billion. In accordance with EU rules, the reimbursement of expenditure incurred by the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund will take place in January 2017 and will contribute to the budget of European funds under the common agricultural policy. Payment of advances of 70% Direct payments will last from 17 October to 30 November. Advances will cover all 17 types of direct payments. Over PLN 14 billion is planned for direct payments for 2016. 1355,000 are applied for. farmers. These measures will come from the European Union. The payment of direct payments starts every year on 1 December and lasts until the end of June in normal mode. It is appropriate to add here that this is only a transfer at the time of payment of the direct payments due to farmers. These measures have neither increased nor been additional aid. These are measures that farmers receive annually from the European Union budget. Only their payout time has changed, which in the event of problems with their payments this year - let us recall here the problem of the ARIMR, which did not cope with the temporary payment of these payments - is worrying whether the institutions responsible for these payments They will cope with this considerable acceleration. I will quote here the words of the Prime Minister, the Deputy Prime Minister. According to his statement, you have decided to help farmers, and it is as if some kind of accounting movement, purely accounting, to shift some funds over time, because the payment of these funds will be compensated in early January by a contribution from the European Union budget. As the Prime Minister said, if the government had not done so, in the normal application process these subsidies would have gone to farmers by April and even by May next year. Given the difficult situation in agriculture, e.g. African swine fever, we want to meet farmers - as the Deputy Prime Minister said. This is quite questionable aid because it is aid already planned by farmers, that is the money that farmers receive each year. Farmers know how they get it, so I don't know what this help is. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The question remains whether the improvement of the work of the authorities and the accounting movement in the budget law deserve the title of aid to farmers, whether it is simply the use of the possibility of improving the efficiency of the transfer of their money to farmers.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u26	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, As a general rule, the Agency pays direct supplies for a given period from 1 December to 30 June of the following year, unless we have such a case as this year that the government for various reasons fails to fulfil this statutory obligation. However, Union rules allow, where justified, advance payments to be paid in advance, i.e. as the Prime Minister said, from 17 October to the end of November. This was the first time the agency took advantage of this opportunity last year. At that time she spent just over 2.5 billion PLN. Part of the holdings were excluded from the advances due to on-the-spot checks or other elements which were previously verified in the applications and as of 17 October Ub. r. could not be completed. This year, almost PLN 9 billion is to be spent on pre-paid advances, as the advances are to be covered by all agricultural holdings. The amount is, of course, secured by European Union funds on the basis of the financial envelope established in July and the area of crops approved on the basis of applications. What's going to be released now will come back to us. As far as I'm concerned, the agency has started paying money into farmers' accounts since Monday. As I understand it, it has secured measures for this purpose and does nothing that would unduly and unauthorizedly increase the deficit of European funds. There is no doubt that the situation on agricultural markets is not easy. Producers of fruit and vegetables, milk or pig farmers are experiencing another difficult year, they are experiencing a crisis. Plus, there are problems with this year's drought. It is also another year in which farmers face problems caused by weather conditions. However, the payment of advances to the extent that this year will take place is the only idea of the current government as to how to help farmers. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u79	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act on Special Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2017, commonly known as the Around Budget Act, is a close complement to the solutions and priorities that were adopted in the 2017 budget plan, which the Prime Minister was just talking about. In order to implement the priorities and plans set out in the draft 2017 plan, it was necessary to make some changes to the law in force in order to properly relocate funds in line with the priorities adopted in the 2017 budget. In addition, in order to carry out the tasks imposed on different state institutions efficiently, it is also necessary to make certain changes to make the activities of these bodies more flexible or to use the resources they have at their disposal or the supervisory bodies of the institutions which have such resources. With regard to the proposals put forward in the around-the-budget bill, I think we should start with changes that are of a substantive nature and substance. These measures include maintaining at the level of the previous years the basis for write-downs to an occupational social benefit fund as a rule for the entire budgetary sphere, except for activities such as higher education. It's also about write-offs for teachers, or pension write-offs for uniformed services, where this fund is different. These base amounts are also maintained there at the level of 2016. Thus, depending on which professional group we are talking about, this will be either 2013 as for university staff, or 2012 as for teachers, and for other professional groups that are employed in budget units it will be the 2010 base. The next amendment, which is linked to the implementation of the budget law, refers to solutions in the biofuel promotion law, according to which 1.5% should be allocated to the promotion of biofuels. planned revenues from the previous year's excise duty on diesel fuels. In this respect, however, this is, of course, a bit of a technical effect. Although the Act contains amendments which I am referring to at the moment, which are of a substantive nature, the implementing regulation, which would allow for the financing of such tasks, has not yet been notified to the European Commission. So, anyway, it would be useless to make such copies for this purpose, because this type of task could not be carried out. The amendment proposed in this Act relating to the implementation of the Budget Act for 2017 consists in the suspension for this year, the non-application of provisions relating to the write-down to the biofuel fund for the promotion of fuel excise revenue. Another change that is proposed in the project is the one-year shift in the ceiling of subsidies for support centres for people with mental disorders. The Act amending the Social Assistance Act in the current year changed the level of funding for participants in support centres for people with mental disorders, adopting as a standard 200% income criterion for a single economy person. However, if we look at it, we will see that the law that was adopted by the High House in 2016 assumed that since 2017 this level of funding would be 230% The income criterion for a single economy person - the income criterion taken into account by the public - and in 2018 this was to be 250% of the income criterion for a single economy person. This Around Budget Act proposes a one-year increase in this level of funding for participants in support centres for people with mental disorders. But if we look at the justification of the project that introduced these regulations, we will see that, in fact, the level of funding for participants in support centres for people with mental disorders has already been achieved, the one that was planned in the draft law introducing these increased levels of support each year. If we look at the justification for the level of funding that the authors of this project assumed, it will actually turn out that what was expected to be achieved as a result of the increase of this threshold to 230% in 2017 was already achieved in 2016. It follows from the justification that this amount per person who is a participant in support centres of people with mental disorders in 2016 was to be PLN 1080, while already this year it is over PLN 1200. This amount was not to be reached until 2017. This change will therefore lead to a level of 200% being maintained in 2017 as well. aid for the financing of the participation of persons in support centres for persons with mental disorders, i.e. the level of 2016. But, as I said just now, this is the level set by project authors actually in 2017, and that is the level we already have. As in previous years, it is assumed that medical and nursing traineeships will be financed by the Labour Fund in 2017. This is over 39 billion PLN, 39.1 billion PLN. Of course, these loans were intended to complement the lack of resources to fulfil the State's obligations to implement the rights to pensions owed to citizens. However, given the perspective, the forward-looking approach with regard to the income of the Social Insurance Fund and the ability to repay this loan, which is not really likely in the nearest time horizon, is proposed here by the High House to cancel this loan. This will in no case affect the level of the deficit of the public finance sector or the level of the government budget deficit, since, of course, earlier these loans which were granted to the Social Insurance Fund were shown in the expenditure of that Social Insurance Fund, as these funds financed pensions and pensions. So that it does not translate - as I just said - into a deficit in the sector or a deficit in the budget. Other solutions are more technical in nature. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u267	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary bill amending the Act on Social Insurance for Farmers, print No 850. The draft law amending the Act on social insurance for farmers aims to increase to a minimum the amount of the possibility to be insured, at the request of the farmer, in the KRUS where the person concerned is covered by ordinary social insurance from the titles of contracts: agency, contract of order or other contract for the provision of services to which, in accordance with the Civil Code, the provisions concerning orders or the performance of functions in the Supervisory Board for remuneration apply. Currently, the farmer or the household may, on application, be covered by social insurance for farmers despite being covered by the general scheme, provided that the remuneration for non-agricultural activities does not exceed 50% the minimum remuneration in the monthly settlement. After this amount has been exceeded, the farmer or the householder shall be subject to double compulsory insurance in the Social Insurance Institution and voluntary insurance in the KRUS. The amount of the lowest remuneration in the universal insurance scheme is the limit amount to which there is an obligation to add together revenues from different sources for the purpose of their compulsory contribution to the Social Security Office. It is therefore appropriate for the farmer to be insured in KRUS up to the minimum wage and only after it has been exceeded will he be insured in KRUS voluntarily upon request. Another area that regulates the proposed bill is the employment of employees in management positions in the organizational cells of the cashier's headquarters, regional branches and field offices. The managers and their alternates shall be appointed and dismissed and the other staff employed under the employment contract. This amendment is intended to enable the President to conduct a rational and effective HR policy in individual KRUS units. It is clear that the director or manager responsible for the operation of his subordinate unit must be able to select his deputy freely. It is therefore necessary to regulate this issue legally in order not to leave room for very different interpretations concerning the taking up of the role of deputies. The third amendment concerns the financial economy of the cash register. It allows free funds of the administrative fund to be placed in bank accounts. This is also the fulfilment of post-control requests, as I mentioned earlier, of the Supreme Audit Chamber. The principle of sound financial management should be a fundamental principle in each budgetary institution and is absolutely supported by law and justice. With regard to the article. 2 the Law and Justice Club presents an amendment which sets out the time to issue implementing rules, i.e. 6 months after the entry into force of the Act. This is Amendment 1. Amendment 2. Art. 3 of the proposed law is replaced by the following: Labour relations of employees employed since the entry into force of this Act in the positions of deputy head of organisational cells in headquarters, regional branches and field offices, and heads of regional branches and their deputies, head of field offices and their deputies referred to in Article. 60 Law referred to in Article. 1, in the wording of this Act, become labour relations on the basis of an appointment within the meaning of the Labour Code of 26 June 1974. Amendment 3. After the article. The following Article shall be inserted: 4 in the same wording as Article. 3 in the submitted project. In conclusion, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft law submitted by the group of Members on the amendment of the Act on Social Insurance for Farmers, print No 850, together with the amendments tabled, and will vote in favour of its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u271	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act on the amendment of the Act on Social Insurance for Farmers regulates staff matters in the Social Insurance Agricultural Fund, clarifying the structure of this entity and allowing for a uniform interpretation of the provisions in question. According to this proposal, the director of the field departments and their deputies and the managers of the field offices and their deputies shall be appointed and dismissed by the President of the KRUS. The Act clarifies the issues relating to the financial economy of the KRUS, indicating that the Regulation of the Minister of Agriculture will also specify the way in which the free resources of the administrative fund are to be allocated, which makes it possible to comply with the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber as a result of the inspection carried out in 2015. This law introduces a significant amendment to the wording of Article. 5b Act on Social Insurance for Farmers. This provision allows insurance in KRUS where a farmer or a householder is covered by social insurance other than under the Act on social insurance for farmers by virtue of an agency contract, contract or other contract for the provision of services or by appointment to the supervisory board. Until now, in order to remain insured in KRUS, income from other activities could not exceed half the minimum wage. The revised adjustment increases this amount, indicating that revenue from other activities must not exceed an amount equal to the minimum wage. Let us look at the legislative history of this provision. Article 5b of the Act on Social Insurance for Farmers was added to that Act by the Act of 23 October 2014. The explanatory memorandum of this law states that it is intended to extend the scope of the Act on social insurance for farmers to persons who, in parallel with the pursuit of agricultural activities, take as additional work under the contract of order, because, in the current legal state, the contract of order, even short-term and low-paid, results in the exclusion from social insurance of farmers under the law and the compulsory coverage of insurance under the general scheme. In general, the problem of farmers who lost their right to insurance in KRUS before 2014 in connection with the execution of low-paid contract orders as conservators and drivers of battle carts of TSOs, chiefs collecting agricultural tax and farmers who by choice were called to represent the professional interests of agricultural producers in supervisory boards of such entities, for example. agricultural cooperatives, agricultural wholesale markets, agri-food and grain and mill enterprises, companies, cooperative banks, as well as the social security fund for farmers of KRUS. These solutions allowed farmers to remain in the agricultural insurance scheme despite their professional activities resulting in compulsory social insurance for appointment to the supervisory board or taking up work under an order contract, agency contract or other service contract. The possibility of remaining in the agricultural social security scheme under this provision is conditioned only by the condition of the amount of income. The Kukiz Poselski Club15 initiates and supports all changes aimed at supporting farmers and providing them with the opportunity to obtain alternative sources of income. Agriculture is a sector of the economy closely linked to human-independent weather conditions. Legislation in this field is also highly susceptible, among others. to changes in the agricultural policy of the countries of the European Union. It is necessary to seek alternative sources of income in the countryside that would ensure a decent life for farmers and their families. Therefore, the Kukiz Club15 will support the proposed law, but carefully looking at the changes taking place in the KRUS. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u295	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the draft law amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, which was included in print No. 884. The Parliamentary bill entered the Sejm for this term of office on 11 December 2015. The use of the wording of the Sejm in this term of office is not accidental because it is identical to the projects which were submitted in the 6th and 7th term of the Sejm. Unfortunately, at the time these projects were not considered positively. The draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund's Pension Act contains proposals to protect the pension benefits of indebted recipients by increasing the amount free of deductions and introducing the principle that the amount of the lowest benefit would already be free from tax deductions. This project does not change the rules for carrying out bail-out, does not introduce abolition of debts, and it really involves two aspects: it concerns indebted pensioners who are executed, but pensioners, renners who have very low benefits. This project does not change the situation of recipients whose benefits are even national average, average benefit, this project of such persons does not apply to any extent. The aim of this project is only to increase the amount free of deduction. The project is modelled on an amount free of deduction for workers, since the minimum wage is already free from bailouts. If the employee earns the current minimum rate, PLN 1850 gross, then the bailiff can no longer carry out executions from this salary and therefore the project was modelled on this situation. The project was a response to the situation that indebted pensioners could receive benefits that constituted less than a minimum of existence. I would like to point out that today's minimum of existence is over 500 PLN per month, and this is an amount below which one can no longer survive. I'm not talking about the minimum social amount, I'm talking about the minimum existence. On the other hand, the lowest pension at present is PLN 880,45 gross, and the lowest pension is PLN 675,13 gross. Only 50% are protected by applicable law in the course of bail-out activities. the lowest amount of pension and pension, so the indebted pensioner, and even more so the pensioner under the current legislation could be deprived of a survivor's benefit, i.e. he could remain for the rest of his life with a monthly amount paid below the minimum of existence. This parliamentary project was addressed by your Marshal on 22 December 2015 to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, where the first reading took place on 13 January and the project was addressed to the Permanent Sub-Committee for detailed consideration. The project was commissioned for appropriate legal analyses at the Sejm Analysis Office, and in August this year. The government of the Republic of Poland has adopted its position on this project. The opinions and positions point to the need to increase legal protection in terms of guaranteeing the benefit, but it is pointed out that increasing it to the full extent of the minimum benefit could not only be detrimental to creditors and unfair to them, but could also be detrimental to the recipients themselves, i.e. indebted pensioners and pensioners. First, pensioners who receive the lowest benefits would lose their lending capacity entirely in banks, but also in the case of the current debtors to whom enforcement proceedings have already been carried out, in the event that enforcement from pension benefits is prevented, the bailiff would be forced to look for other sources of execution and would, for example, be carried out. to more frequent real estate executions. Certainly not such a pseudo-correction of the situation of pensioners was concerned with applicants. Given that next year the lowest pension will increase significantly, and therefore the lowest pension will increase to PLN 1000, i.e. by more than 10%. , a dozen per cent - an amendment was accepted during the work of the subcommittee and proposed in the article. 141 Social Insurance Fund pension law adding point 1a, which would provide that pensions are free from executions and deductions in a proportion corresponding to 75% the amount of the lowest pension, i.e. we have reached a compromise. Transitional provisions have also been amended to provide that the reduction-free amount is increased from 50 to 75% the lowest provision is also applicable to the executions already being carried out. Vacatio legis has also been amended, stating that the Act will enter into force on the first day of the month following the expiry of six months from the date of publication. The report of the Sub-Committee on these amendments was adopted. The subcommittee adopted them on 13 September this year, and on 4 October the subcommittee's report was examined at the committee meeting and the committee proposes that the new law be adopted in such a form. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u296	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Halina Szydleko, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u297	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice on amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund, print No. 884. The subject of this amendment, which concerns an increase in the amount free from seizure in the event of an execution for pension benefits, has already been taken up several times by the Sejm of its previous term of office. This is a very important topic because it concerns a group of the least wealthy, often living on the verge of existence. Pension benefits are subject to enforcement and any pensioner who becomes a debtor should consider that part of his benefits may be taken up in the course of enforcement proceedings. The problem is how big is it supposed to be? The amount free of occupation should guarantee the pensioner a minimum income to meet the basic needs. This is not the case at present. In the case of the lowest pensions and pensions, the bailiff may take up to 50% of this benefit. This is a much smaller security than e.g. in the case of remuneration for work. Thus, pension benefits are not sufficiently protected. Even from the lowest pension or pension, the bailiff can execute, which results in people receiving these benefits being left without means of living, and this leads to dramatic situations for those who, as a result of this execution, are not only unable to pay back debts that are still increasing due to interest and costs of execution, but also do not have enough resources, they have nothing to live for. One might wonder if it would be right for an amount free of occupation to be equal to the lowest pension or pension benefit, but sometimes execution would be impossible. True, the creditor could satisfy other assets of the debtor, but often these other assets simply do not exist. This would also be against a pensioner who would not have been able to credit, since in such a case no bank would have granted credit to a person who does not have collateral and no claim on his benefits could be enforced. The interest of the creditor, which cannot be forgotten, is also important. It is therefore appropriate to set an amount free of seizure at 75% , not, as previously proposed, 100%. Thus, in the case of the proposed change, the pensioner receiving the lowest benefit could receive about 600 PLN, while the pensioner could receive 400 PLN. And that's the minimum existence. It should also be noted that this regulation will not reduce the enforcement of the claim, but only extend the recovery period, while leaving sufficient resources to pensioners for a decent life. With this in mind, the Law and Justice Club fully supports this project. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The proposed solution aims to provide pensioners with similar protection, which workers are entitled to at the enforcement stage as regards remuneration for work. I would remind you that the original draft proposed that this amount should be free of the full lowest or minimum pension benefit. These arguments, which we discussed today, namely the pursuit of preservation by those receiving the lowest pension benefits of credit, have decided that, in the official position of the Council of Ministers presented to the Sejm Marshal on 2 August 2016, the government was in favour of setting this amount free of deductions at 70%. the lowest pension. With regard to the other proposals that were included in the original proposal, it was precisely the desire to protect creditors that our position in this regard was negative. As regards the questions that were raised during the discussion, I would also like to draw attention to one thing, namely the draft law amending the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, which is currently underway within the government. I mean a project to increase the amount of the lowest pension benefits. I will remind you that this project assumes that the lowest pension benefits are to increase from PLN 882 to PLN 1000, while the lowest pension benefits, the lowest benefits for partial incapacity for work according to this project are to increase to PLN 750. I would like to say that these are actions that are heading in the same direction as the draft law that is being discussed today amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law. They aim to protect the poorest pensioners and pensioners. The solutions introduced to this second draft law will also apply to those affected by enforcement proceedings, because an increase in the amounts of the lowest pension benefits will automatically increase the deductions-free amounts of the various possible deductions. With regard to the questions raised in the course of the discussion on the scope of the impact of the draft law discussed today, I would like to say that this project will apply to pension benefits in a situation where enforcement is carried out both under the rules on administrative execution and under the rules on civil enforcement. With regard to enforcement rules, the rules governing executions from cash apply here, so that detailed arrangements are contained in the regulations on appropriate enforcement procedures. As regards the improvement of the quality of life of what number of people will contribute to the introduction of this project, we do not have this type of data, because while we are, of course, able to determine the number of people receiving the lowest pension benefits, it is not possible to determine the number of pension benefits that are subject to enforcement under both the rules on enforcement in the administration and the rules on civil execution. As regards the issue of the introductory provision discussed today, I would like to say that the assumption is that this law will concern both the commitments that have been made, i.e. loans which have been incurred before the date of entry into force of the Law and the commitments already being executed under the rules of both civil and administrative execution, as well as those commitments that will be made in the future, after the provisions of the Act discussed today have entered into force. We have the position of the Legislative Office on this issue, which was presented during the work of both the subcommittee and the committee, and as if we share the legitimacy of using such a specific introductory provision. I would also like to say that the bill assumes a relatively long vacathio legis, because it is practically a 6-month vacathio legis, so that people to whom the provisions of this law will apply will, according to our assessment, have an adequate amount of time to comply with its rules. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u331	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the government's draft law amending the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist and certain other laws. This draft law as a print No 769 was addressed to the first reading in the Health Committee. This reading took place on 22 September this year. The committee's report is in print No 898. Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of this project is to restore post-graduate internship as a necessary step in the professional development of a doctor and dentist enabling practical apprenticeships in a harmonised legal and organisational framework. As is known, a postgraduate period for doctors and dentists was abandoned in 2011 based on the assumption that the introduction of practical training for the 6th year of studies would be a sufficient equivalent for this period to reduce the duration of training for professionals and speed up their access to the labour market. On the other hand, at a later stage, it was noted that students who are being trained in a new way, who will have two semesters of practical training in clinical fields in their studies, would be deprived of formal entitlement to health services due to a lack of right to pursue their profession, which was considered to be a serious problem. This has become one of the important reasons for abandoning this way of education. The first graduates, who were to be trained in a new way, were to leave medical schools in 2018. This means that so far there has been virtually no precedent for student training without a post-graduate internship. The proposed act will therefore not be another change imposed on a slightly earlier change, but on the contrary, it will stabilise the previously existing system for many years, which was a good system, tested and will now develop in the right direction. The full right to pursue the profession provided by the proposed law to trainees, taking into account the possibility of issuing prescriptions and issuing opinions and medical certificates, ensures, on the one hand, the high usefulness of trainees operating in the healthcare system, increasing in fact the number of doctors on the labour market, on the other hand, the assignment of a trainee to a specialist in a particular field of medicine or a dental practitioner pursuing a profession for at least 5 years, will ensure that trainees enter into professional life and improve practical skills so important in working with the patient. For these reasons, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this project in the vote, and for my part I would like to say: Ladies and gentlemen, I am a cardiologist, I am a dormitory, a professor of medicine, and for several decades I have been teaching medical students, teaching interns, doctors specialising in medicine, and I also know what is said in academic circles on this subject. It is clear that the common problem of current education is too little practical education in different directions, including medicine. This practicality of medicine is very important. In medicine, a very important issue is the issue of patient contact - doctor, relationship building, humanization preventing the dehumanization of the profession, these are extremely important problems. In this connection, I am personally pleased by the sequence of events that have taken place, as you will note that the proposed changes in the liquidation of the traineeship have forced changes in the medical training system that have put medical studies into practice. The projects converted many hours of seminars into exercises. What we would simply like to see happen is included in the education provisions. It was too little to replace a post-graduate internship, and it was simply not good in itself, of course, but if it was supplemented by an internship that will be restored, then, ladies and gentlemen, we have a real change in the right direction, because on the one hand, it was practicalized in medical studies, and on the other hand, a post-graduate internship was saved. So basically, net, a doctor's student will be able to do more after a post-graduate internship, which I'm very happy about. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u340	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! This will enable them to acquire the necessary practical skills and to deepen their theoretical knowledge. The project assumes that a person who has completed his or her medical studies will receive the right to pursue his or her profession for the duration of his post-graduate internship. We have no doubt that an internship has a positive role to play in the profession of a doctor and dentist. It is necessary to complement knowledge and skills and allows the safe entry of a young doctor into the profession. The project also includes changes to the rules on exams, access to tests for these exams, the situation of foreigners wishing to take a post-graduate course. Unfortunately, there is nothing in the project about raising salaries for both intern doctors and, above all, for resident doctors. Without a clear increase in wages for even doctors residents who have paid worse than in Biedronka, we will not increase the number of doctors practicing in Poland. Our country against the background of European countries is at the gray end in terms of the number of doctors per 1000 inhabitants. Without their fair pay, we will train doctors who will pursue themselves professionally in countries that appreciate their work. That is why we call for our amendment to be adopted and for the unfair remuneration of resident doctors to be stopped. In this draft law, applicants propose to introduce a number of changes in the field of training in the profession of doctors and dentists which have a positive impact on the quality of teaching in these professions. Recognizing the purpose of all the changes made, I will refer only to the first ones. The main proposed regulation is the restoration of post-graduate internship for doctors and dentists, which was abandoned by the 2011 amendment of the Law. According to the projectors, post-graduate training is an important element after post-graduate education and plays an important role in strengthening and enhancing the practical skills of young doctors. The key to this solution is the fact that doctors internships on time and for post-graduate training will be granted full right to pursue their profession. Allowing young doctors to perform such activities as issuing prescriptions or issuing opinions or medical certificates will help to make more effective use of their competences and extend the possibility for patients to access medical services. It is also important to supervise an intern's doctor who, as amended, will be held by a physician with a medical professional or a dental practitioner who has a profession of at least 5 years. Another important change in the system of training for doctors and dentists proposed in the procedated project is the introduction of teaching under simulated clinical conditions as a compulsory part of pre-diploma training carried out at all higher education institutions in the 6th year of medical and dental studies and the 5th year of medical and dental studies. The provisions introducing such a form of teaching at all universities in which medical faculties are or are being established will have a beneficial effect on the level of knowledge and development of the skills of future doctors and dentists and, without additional burden on universities, will contribute to progress using modern teaching methods. I consider the possibility that students of the last year of their studies may be excluded from the medical final exam and the medical and dental final exam as another correct amendment proposed in this amendment. This regulation is supported by the title nature of these examinations and by the fact that they constitute one of the grounds for obtaining the right to pursue the profession, which only medical graduates should be entitled to. The greatest controversy in the project is raised by provisions concerning the publicisation of questions, together with the answers in the field of medical specialization and final exams. According to the amendment, the questions and answers in question will be available to those taking the exams shortly after they have been written. However, it will be access. only on a personal basis, without being able to copy. Full publication of the examination material will take place 5 years after the examination. For the benefit of patients in particular, medical training should be at the highest level. It is well known that the current way of verifying doctors' knowledge and skills does not fully meet all expectations. The total publicisation of questions and answers from examinations immediately after they have been carried out may have a negative impact on the extent of the material adopted by the persons taking the exam. In view of the limited resources and opportunities involved in the creation of new examination kits, this risk is even more real. Nobody should care about lowering the level of Polish medicine. Given the number of benefits resulting from the introduction of the procedated project, the Free and Solidarnia Circle will vote in favour of its further procession. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u362	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the Act on Individual Income Tax. This report is contained in print No 901 and the draft law concerned is in print No 794. On 2 September, the Marshal of the Sejm referred the draft to the first reading held at the meeting of the Sejm on 13 September, while the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development considered the draft on 5 October and unanimously adopted the report without any amendments. The purpose of the amendment is to create the head of the district office or the head of the regional branch in which there has been cumulation of certain tasks, legal opportunities to benefit from the assistance of employees employed in another district office or regional department who carry out similar tasks and are not overly burdened with work. This will make better use of the Agency's staff resources and contribute to faster processing of farmers' applications. Another change to improve the agency's operation is an increase from 15 000. PLN up to 40,000. zł amount above which the approval of the Minister of Finance is required to redeem the claim. It is worth noting that this amount has not been valued since 2003. The amendment will also enable the President of the Agency to waive his claims ex officio where the claim has not been recovered as a result of the winding-up or insolvency proceedings carried out and where there is a reasonable presumption that the enforcement procedure will not obtain an amount higher than the costs of the recovery and enforcement of the claim. In addition, the amendment clarifies the way in which the amounts unduly or unduly collected by the company or members of a civil partnership which has been dissolved can be recovered from the debtor's ledger in respect of the appropriations from the budget of the European Union and from the national budget by interrupting the limitation period until the end of the enforcement procedure. The provisions of the draft law also provide a legal basis for deductions of outstanding amounts from the Structural Funds co-financed by the European Union and national public funds to co-finance expenditure from European funds. The amendment also introduces an exemption from income tax on natural persons for the amounts of decommitted claims. The proposed changes are necessary and do not raise any doubts, which is why on behalf of the committee I would like the High Sejm to adopt a government bill amending the Act on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture and the Act on Income Tax on Individuals. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u370	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the pleasure of presenting my position on behalf of the Poselski Club on the government draft law amending the Act on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the Act on Income Tax on Individuals, included in print No. 794, the committee's report is contained in print No. 901. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft Law of 9 May 2008 on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture presented by the Government of Law and Justice aims to introduce changes that rationalise the functioning of the Agency. The detailed solutions contained in the submission were the subject of our in-depth analysis. The provisions enabling the President of the Agency to waive its claims ex officio should the claims laid down in the Act not be recovered and where there is a reasonable suspicion that enforcement costs will not be covered as a result of enforcement. The submission also contains legal provisions enabling the management of the county offices and regional branches of the agencies to benefit from the assistance of employees employed in another county office or regional department who carry out similar tasks. This is a good direction, in the modern assessment worth considering even on the scale of the entire administration, of course following reliable and multi-directional analyses. The proposal presented to the High House also provides for an increase from 15 to 40 000. zł amount above which the approval of the Minister of Finance is required to redeem the claim. After the explanations provided by the Ministry, we considered this to be a legitimate change, although of course it initially aroused our interest due to the concern for the state of public finances of modern times. The draft also contains provisions that clearly govern the way in which amounts unduly or unduly collected payments are made by a company or members of a civil partnership which has been dissolved. We consider this to be the right direction, too. The High House should adopt rules that meet the requirement of ambiguity, as I would remind you that the ambiguity of the law is one of the biggest problems that citizens who have to deal with the law on a daily basis. The submission shall also include provisions allowing the recovery of the amounts entered in the debtor's ledger in respect of the appropriations from the budget of the European Union and the national budget by interrupting the limitation period until the date of termination of the enforcement procedure. This provision will allow the limitation period to be interrupted in order to recover the debts included in the debtor's ledger. By looking at economic debts that are de facto blocked by the possibility of faster, more efficient development, let us consider the provision to be rational. The project also proposes the creation of a legal basis for deductions of outstanding claims from European funds, e.g. from a financial instrument to support fisheries, but also from other instruments and national public funding to co-finance expenditure from European funds, e.g. from the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund, but also from other instruments. This could, of course, raise doubts, but this mechanism enshrined in the Act and communicated to all concerned will certainly contribute to improving financial management and improving the flow of funds. The draft also provides for exemption from income tax on individuals from the amounts of deferred claims referred to in Article. 24 of the Act of 9 May 2008 on the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture. This is, of course, the consequence of the specific nature of the matter covered by this legislation. The Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, considering the draft law submitted to the High House, has not made any amendments. It seems that as of today, all clubs agree with this project as substantive and purposeful. To sum up, the Modern Posel Club at this stage will also not make any amendments and will support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u392	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'm answering questions about the bill we're working on. It's $40,000. In general, it must be said that these are emergency situations and quite precisely defined: if no debt has been recovered in the case of winding-up or insolvency proceedings, if it is assumed that enforcement proceedings will not obtain an amount higher than the costs of the investigation, if the debtor's residence and residence cannot be determined - I read it briefly - if the execution threatens the existence of the debtor. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u393	Thank you. I would like the floor of the rapporteur for the committee of Mr Zbigniew Dolata. No. Okay, thank you very much. The discussion is closed. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u394	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! On behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I present a draft law amending the Payment Act under direct support schemes, print No 879. The first reading of this project took place at the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 6 October. After this examination, the committee requests the adoption of a draft law and presents the relevant report to the High House in print no. 911. The project includes amendments to the Act of 5 February 2015 on payments under direct support schemes. These changes result from an analysis by the Ministry of Agriculture of the functioning of the new direct support scheme and from the need to adapt sugar beet payments to the new conditions for granting them due to the abolition of quota sugar production on 1 October 2017. This analysis of the functioning of the new system during these periods is consistent with the reservations that the European Commission has submitted to the new direct support system that operates in Poland. Therefore, in August this year the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development notified the European Commission of the new solutions contained in the project. In addition, the 2013 Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union provided for the possibility for Member States to review their direct support schemes, of which the Polish Government has benefited by presenting the draft law under discussion today. The amendments introduced by the Act concern protein crops, soft fruit, sugar beet, cattle, cows, sheep and goats, as well as support for small farms and the conditions and arrangements for granting advances on direct payments. Now, High Izbo, I will discuss the proposed changes one at a time. In place of existing payments to protein crops, it is proposed to introduce two separate forms of support, i.e. payments to legumes for grain and payments to fodder plants. With this change, the existing support potential will be better exploited in this segment. As regards changes in soft fruit payments, due to the improvement of the cost-effectiveness of raspberries production, it is proposed to move away from supporting this crop. The strawberries will, of course, continue to be supported. In the case of sugar beet payments, the change is to depart from the principle of granting payments only to the area so called. quota crops for the total area covered by the contract. The changes in payments to bovine animals and cows consist in reducing the limit of animals on the holding to be covered by the payment from 30 to 20 units. This change is in line with the support policy recommended by the European Commission in this sector. Further changes concern payments to sheep, the change of the date from autumn to spring, the holding of these animals in order to improve the management of the herd and better on-the-spot checks carried out by the paying agency. Further amendments concern simplification of applications. These are data from the computerised database of goats and sheep, which are used for the purpose of the application. Further amendment concerns small farms. This is the case where the amount of support does not exceed EUR 1250. The changes relate to the calculation of the amount of support. The next amendment concerns those small farms which, following the acquisition of a decrease or acquisition of another holding, will exceed the said support limit of EUR 1250. In this case, it will also be possible to withdraw from the scheme for small farms after 30 September of the year of application. The penultimate amendment facilitates the agency's control activities by obliging farmers to provide the necessary assistance to the controller. The last amendment proposed in this project is the amendment which concerns advances paid to farmers for direct payments so that there is no unjustified payment of these advances. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At the first reading of this draft law on 6 October, the members of the committee agreed to accept the proposed amendments, for which I warmly thank you on behalf of the committee. Once again at this point, I request that the High House adopt a draft law amending the Payment Act under direct support schemes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u404	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I am pleased to present my position on the draft law amending the Payment Act under direct support schemes. The aim of the project is to amend the rules for granting payments in sectors of particular economic importance whose current profitability is insufficient. This sector, which is very important for many sectors of agriculture and the agri-food industry, the potential of which is underestimated, is high protein crops. Other forms of support are proposed in the new solution, i.e. payments to protein crops for grain legumes payments and payments to fodder plants. This is a move in the right direction, but too conservative. It will not allow the potential of Polish agriculture to be exploited. I believe that there is a need to step up work to reduce the use of GMOs. It is therefore necessary to increase the supply of Polish protein used in feed. During the first reading of the draft of this Act, the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development discussed the amount of support for grain legumes farmers. The Minister stated that the payments would be degressive, i.e. a higher, 100% rate of aid for the first 75 hectares of crops on the farm, and above these 75 hectares would be a lower rate, 50%. At this point, I would like to make it clear that I do not agree with the reduction of grain legumes payments to 50% of the amount of aid above the area of 75 hectares. It is necessary to increase the area of crops to which aid for protein crops will be granted in full, so that feed producers cannot explain the lack of possibility to purchase plants due to too small, uneven lots. I therefore ask the Minister to increase the area of crops to which 100% will be granted payment, up to 150 ha. The increase in the area of crops on the holding to which a full payment may be granted for legume crops for sowing may be considered necessary as an action to adapt grain supply to the potential demand of feed-producing plants. The actions proposed by me will also lead to the fact that in the hands of Polish farmers will remain the money that is currently being sent to the USA, Brazil or Argentina for soy shots of GMOs. And this height is really high, because it is over PLN 4 billion a year. These are measures that should remain in Polish agriculture, increasing the profitability of agricultural holdings. During the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 19 May on the use of protein crops in feedingstuffs, a representative of the Ministry of Agriculture informed that imports into Poland of GMO soybeans, the main component of feed, are currently 2 to 3 million t per year. Judging by the solutions proposed by the Proceded Law, it will be difficult to meet the demand for protein crops up to the indicated height, which is necessary to ensure independence from imported GMO soybeans and to create a self-sufficient system of fodder legumes. It is also necessary to increase the envelope intended to support protein crops, as by reviewing the profitability of individual crops whose production is supported by direct payments, it can be concluded that the cost-effectiveness of legumes is very low. To sum up: increasing the area of crops and increasing the subsidies will ensure food security of the country and, on the other hand, increase the profitability of agriculture by leaving at least PLN 4 billion in the pockets of Polish farmers. Finally, I would like to remind you, particularly from the Committee on Agriculture, that it is necessary to speed up the work on the draft law on the use of protein crops in feedingstuffs that has already been drawn up, so that soon abroad [[♪ The bell ♪]] corporations could or wanted to use high protein plants from Polish fields. The Kukiz Club15 will vote in favour of the proposed wording of the law, hoping to take account of the solutions proposed by me. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u405	Thank you very much. [[Well done, Mr President.]] Mrs Ewa Lieder, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u406	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The bill responds to the concerns of the European Commission regarding the part of the payments for production used in Poland in 2015 and 2016. The main changes proposed in the draft concern the abolition of the raspberry aid - soft fruit aid is to be granted only for strawberries - covering the entire EU sugar beet area, aid for 20 cows and not, as yet, up to 30 cows, grain legumes and fodder plant subsidies (only up to 75 hectares), simplifying the application procedure for support for sheep and goat farming (no animal identification numbers are required with the application). Under current conditions, holding with a number of 30 bovine animals it is still a small farm, we do not see any justification for increasing support for even smaller farms, and we believe that this limit should not be lowered. With regard to the consequences - milk producers will surely lose on these changes. The direction in the European Union is clearly defined in this case and leads to a reduction in milk production. The abolition of raspberry subsidies according to the Podkarpackie Agricultural Chamber may result in the liquidation of many plantations in the area. Of course, changes to subsidies cannot safeguard the interests of all parties, but it is important that changes to the directly concerned social group are not constructed entirely without their participation. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u410	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I will present our position on the government draft law amending the Payment Act under direct support schemes, contained in print No. 879. Today's bill introduces changes to direct support instruments for Polish agriculture. The government had to modify the payment calculation system in 2017 after taking into account the trend of production in 2014. The Payment Act verified the situation of individual sectors. According to the Regulation, production-related support can only be granted to those sectors of agricultural production which are particularly important for economic, social or environmental reasons and are in difficulty. The bill provides for changes in payments to protein crops. Protein crops are to be used as a basis for the production of feed as a result of this change. I count on what was also emphasized during the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development that Polish farmers will grow legumes and use them for the production of feed. This gives us an opportunity to open up a new market in this area. The changes were also made in the young cattle and cows, sheep and soft fruit and beet sector. The worrying thing about this law is that the government did not take care of the opinion of important agricultural organisations. It seems that this would allow for a more holistic view of the case. For me, non-agricultural people, it is surprising that the Act does not place a greater emphasis on small farms for which subsidies would be an important supporting element. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u436	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the finance committee, I have the honour to present a government draft law amending the excise tax law - a project aimed at implementing the law of the European Union. The draft concerns the introduction of a solution adapting the excise tax law to the EU requirements concerning excise exemption for coal products. This change is closely linked to the European Commission's condition for Poland's wider application of exemption from excise duty on coal products. After the amendment, energy-intensive plants using coal products for fuel purposes, if they still want to benefit from excise duty relief, will have to have systems related to environmental protection or increasing energy efficiency. At its 27th meeting on 4 October 2016, the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Public Finances for consideration. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u437	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marcin has the floor... [[A dumpster.]] ... Leopard, I'm sorry, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u438	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the excise duty law. The aim of this project is to implement European Union law, as the European Commission has questioned the scope of excise duty exemptions for coal products used by energy-intensive undertakings and by economic operators in which a system has been put into effect to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. The provisions in this respect were introduced by the Law of 16 September 2011 on the reduction of certain obligations of citizens and entrepreneurs. In the view of the European Commission, the exemption from the tax on coal products in these cases is wider than that provided for in the relevant Directive and it is therefore necessary to amend it. No amendments were made to the draft law in the Committee on Public Finances. The Legislative Office has been authorised to make a punctuation correction. It is worth noting that the entry into force of the new rules should contribute to the improvement of the environment, as the new regulations will motivate energy-intensive operators to take action to reduce the negative impact of their activities on the environment. This aspect is also part of the intensive activities undertaken by the Ministry of the Environment. In view of the above, the Law and Justice Club will support the draft law amending the excise duty Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u439	Thank you very much. Monika Wielichowska, on behalf of the Civic Platform, put her position in writing. Please, Mr Mirosław Pampuch, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u440	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the government draft law amending the excise tax law, prints no. 849 and 902. This draft law restores the excise duty exemptions introduced by the Law of 16 September 2011 on the reduction of certain obligations of citizens and businesses. Exemption from excise duty was introduced for activities subject to taxation which are subject to coal products intended for heating purposes. This exemption has been defined to a wider extent than is apparent from the Article. 17 Council Directive 2003/96/EC of 27 October 2003 restructuring the Community framework for the taxation of energy products and electricity, thus calling into question this exemption by the European Union. This proposal will therefore be amended by Article. 31a Act on excise duty. This amendment will exempt energy-intensive undertakings which have a system in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency from excise duty exemption. On the other hand, operators who are energy-intensive but do not have a system in place to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency will have to introduce a system to benefit from excise duty relief, to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. In view of the need to adapt national tax law on indirect tax, namely excise duty, to Community law, the Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of this amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u441	Thank you. Ms Genowefa Tokarska, on behalf of the Polish People's Party, made her position in writing. Please take the floor of Mr Ireneus profit, free and solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u442	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Excise Tax Act, prints No. 849 and 902. The draft law aims to complement the implementation of Council Directive 2003/96/EC of 27 October 2003 restructuring the Community framework for the taxation of energy products and electricity. The provisions of this Directive were introduced into the Polish system of law by the Law of 16 September 2011 on the reduction of certain obligations of citizens and entrepreneurs, but the European Commission questioned the scope of exemptions from excise duty on coal products used by energy-intensive plants and by economic operators in which a system was put in place to achieve environmental objectives or to improve energy efficiency. In the European Commission's view, the tax exemptions introduced in these cases are more extensive than those provided for in the individual directive. In view of the European Commission's position as legitimate, the amendments referred to in the draft law should be made. However, it is worth noting that the proposed changes will affect economic operators who are not energy-intensive entities and energy-intensive entities not complying with the requirements of the Directive. The amendment of the Act will allow the continued use of exemptions for energy-intensive undertakings following the introduction of organisational systems enabling them to achieve environmental objectives or to increase energy efficiency. According to this idea, redundancies also operate in other countries of the European Union. The entry into force of the bill submitted will contribute to the improvement of the environment. The regulation will motivate its addressees to take action to reduce their negative impact on the environment. The date of entry into force of the amendment of the Act is scheduled for 1 July 2017, but this deadline may not be sufficient for energy-intensive operators to implement arrangements authorising the use of excise duty exemptions due to the scope and effects of the regulation. This should prompt the High House to consider extending this deadline. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports initiatives aimed at harmonising European law, all the more so with the objective of protecting the environment. At the same time, I would like to express my expectation that the Government of the Republic of Poland will not leave those who lose the right to exemption from excise duty to themselves during the adjustment period, as the addition to the implementation of the provisions of this Directive may be made at the expense of the final recipients of the products of those entities. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u457	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Convention No 29 on Forced and Compulsory Labour is one of the 8 basic conventions, as has already been said, of the International Labour Organisation and is considered to be an instrument for the protection of fundamental human rights. Although more than 85 years have passed since the Convention was adopted and the ratification of the Convention is almost universal, the International Labour Organisation points out that some 21 million people worldwide are currently victims of forced labour. It is scandalous that this practice still exists despite the wide scope of this convention and the measures taken by the Member States. That is why the International Labour Organisation considered it necessary to adopt complementary measures concerning shortcomings in the application of this Convention. The purpose of the protocol is to develop measures to prevent forced labour and to protect and support victims. The International Labour Organisation, when adopting the Protocol, has set itself the objective of resolutely strengthening the efforts of its members to eliminate modern forms of slavery. As stated by the International Labour Organisation, the new instrument adapts the Convention to contemporary challenges, mainly addressing trafficking in human beings. The attached recommendations provide technical guidance on the implementation process of the new regulations. The Protocol to Convention No 29 promotes and implements fundamental human rights, decent work for all and eliminates trafficking in human beings and effectively eliminates all forms of forced labour. This Protocol is addressed in the Council Decisions adopted on 10 November 2015, which empower the Member States of the European Union to ratify, in the interest of the Union, the Protocol in question as regards matters relating to judicial cooperation in criminal matters and social policy respectively. This makes it possible to take direct action in this regard by adopting complementary measures on gaps in the application of Convention 29. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u459	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We are discussing the Protocol to Convention 29, which was adopted on 11 June by the General Conference of the International Labour Conference. It would seem that this convention, and in particular this protocol, Poland may not apply. We all know that it is estimated that more than 21 million people in the world are victims of forced labour. We are also dealing with a wide range of Convention 29 and with measures taken by individual Member States where it is considered necessary to adopt complementary measures. I would first of all like to focus on some of the most important issues in this speech. Firstly, the definition of forced labour, which is contained in International Labour Organisation Convention No 29. This definition of forced labour says that it is all the work or services required of a person under the threat of any penalty and to which the person concerned has not volunteered. It is also very often the case that forced labour is a result of trafficking in human beings, and trafficking in human beings involves transporting people, usually across borders, to use them. Nevertheless, a key element of this crime is the exercise of control over a person, which allows him to be used, even with the consent of the victim. I would like to point out that this acquisition of control over an employee results in a violation of human rights, and this is the factor that characterizes forced labour and makes it possible to distinguish between this phenomenon and the violation of labour rights. Ladies and gentlemen, now research. We have very interesting research, which was carried out in 2010 by the Centre for Human Trade Research at the University of Warsaw. The phenomenon of forced labour is not foreign to Poland, nor is it foreign to Polish citizens. Among the victims of forced labour in Poland according to these studies are dominated by foreigners from Eastern European countries, mainly Ukraine. More and more people are also identified as victims of forced labour in the Far East countries, especially North Korea, China, Bangladesh, Taiwan, the Philippines, Azerbaijan and Vietnam. The vast majority of victims do not speak Polish. Sadly, Poles are also victims of forced labour. They were mostly used on plantations in Italy and Spain. We therefore believe that it is necessary to adopt this protocol. Civic Platform Club [[♪ The bell ♪]] He'll be in favour of the bill. Thank you very much.
