ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u461	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The issue of forced labour was one of the issues raised during the 103rd session of the International Labour Conference held in Geneva from 28 May to 12 June 2014. Participants decided that members should take additional measures to prevent forced labour. It has been decided to adopt a binding Protocol to Convention No 29 on Forced and Compulsory Labour together with recommendations for the implementation of the Protocol. Following discussions and amendments on 11 June 2014, the minutes were adopted by government delegates, workers and employers by 237 votes to 27 abstentions and 8 against. By adopting the protocol, the aim was to resolutely strengthen the efforts of members to eliminate modern forms of slavery. The new mechanisms adapt Convention 29 on Forced and Compulsory Labour of 1930 to the modern world and mainly concern trafficking in human beings. The attached recommendations provide technical guidance on the implementation process of the new regulations. States are required to ensure effective measures against forced labour and protection and access to support for people exploited. In addition, each country must develop, in consultation with employers' and workers' organisations, national policies and action plans to combat forced and compulsory labour. The regulations planned in the process are modelled on similar regulations on trafficking in human beings. As we read in the explanatory memorandum of Convention No 29, the States are not committed to criminalising the conduct of forced labour and compulsory labour. The Convention only introduces the obligation of its members to abolish forced or compulsory labour, and this effect cannot always and must be implemented by criminal measures. Polish law of this kind provides for such measures. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u462	Thank you very much. Mr Mirosław Pampuch, Modern.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u463	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Protocol to Convention No 29 on Forced Labour of 1930, adopted in Geneva on 11 June 2014, prints No 848 and 896. The Protocol to Convention No 29 was adopted at the 103rd session of the General Conference of the International Labour Conference on 11 June 2014. Under the ratification protocol, the State undertakes to take effective measures to prevent and eliminate the use of forced labour, to provide victims with protection and access to appropriate and effective corrective measures, such as compensation, and to punish the perpetrators of forced or compulsory labour. In addition, the ratifying country receiving the Protocol will develop national policies and action plans in consultation with employers' and workers' organisations for the effective and sustainable eradication of forced or compulsory labour. The ratification of the Protocol does not require or require national law to be brought into line with its provisions. Nor will it increase or reduce the revenues of public finance operators in relation to the size of existing legislation. Furthermore, the Protocol does not extend or restrict the rights and freedoms of the Constitution but merely confirms them. For these reasons, the Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of the ratification of this protocol. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u465	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club our position on the report of the committee on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Protocol to Convention No 29 on Forced Labour of 1930, adopted in Geneva on 11 June 2014. The problem of forced labour was one of the most important issues raised during the 103rd session of the International Labour Conference held in Geneva from 28 May to 12 June 2014. Delegates have decided that the most appropriate instrument for stepping up ILO members' efforts to prevent forced labour will be the legally binding Protocol to Convention 29 on Forced and Compulsory Labour, together with recommendations for the implementation of the Protocol. Following discussion and amendment, the minutes were adopted on 11 June 2014 by delegates from governments, employers and workers. The purpose of the protocol is to develop measures to prevent forced labour and protect and support victims. In adopting the protocol, the ILO has set itself the objective of decisively strengthening the efforts of its members to eliminate modern forms of slavery. Countries have been required to ensure effective measures against forced labour and to ensure protection and access to support for exploiters, including the possibility of receiving compensation, and to introduce appropriate sanctions for perpetrators. In addition, each country must develop, in consultation with employers' and workers' organisations, a national policy and action plan to combat forced and compulsory labour. The regulations proposed in the protocol adopted and the recommendations supporting it, in particular as regards the rights of victims and their protection and support, are modelled on similar rules on trafficking in human beings. The signatory countries also committed themselves to educational and information activities among workers and employers, to ensuring that regulations on prevention of forced labour cover all areas of the economy and to protect immigrants as particularly vulnerable to slave labour, and to adopt measures to identify, protect and support victims and to ensure that they are able to recover and rehabilitate. These projects concern the alignment of national regulations with European Union law on payment transactions using payment cards. The projects introduce new supervisory procedures for electronic payments, the division of supervisory powers over payment instruments between the Financial Supervision Commission and the President of the National Bank of Poland and in their second part so-called. a general payment account intended to improve the participation of adult Poles and Poles in the payment accounts sector. Today's so-called level. Bankruptcy of the Polish society is about 80% and is one of the lowest in Europe. According to the project, any citizen who has not yet had a payment account in any bank or cooperative credit and savings bank will be free of charge to set up such an account and gain access to basic transactions such as deposits and withdrawals, transfer orders or payment orders. Within a month, 15 such free transactions will be possible. Once this number is exceeded, the service provider will, of course, be able to charge a fee, but no higher than most often used in personal accounts. In addition, bank service providers will be required to provide transparent information on fees related to their services, thereby increasing the choice of payment services available to consumers and improving the quality and transparency of services provided by payment service providers, as well as increasing consumer mobility and facilitating comparison of fees and payment account services. At its Tuesday meeting, the Committee on Public Finance accepted the projects together with the amendments. The Modern Posel Club, like the committee, recommends and appreciates this project positively. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u484	Thank you. Ms Genowefa Tokarska, Polish People's Party, has the floor. Excuse me, Madame, you have delivered your position in writing. In that case, Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u485	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Circle of the Members of the Free and Solidarnia, I present my position towards government draft laws amending the Payment Services Act and certain other laws, print Nos 626 and 785, and the reports of the Public Finance Commission, print No 927. On the one hand, these projects aim to align national regulations with European Union legal standards on payment services and on the other hand to create a new framework for advanced financial services which can significantly affect the way in which Polish society makes non-cash payments. The proposed solutions address the need to increase the competitiveness of payment services available to consumers and to improve the quality and transparency of services provided by payment service providers. The requirement to increase consumer mobility makes it necessary to facilitate the availability of payment services and to compare their costs. The implementation of the provisions of Regulation 2015/751 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 29 April 2015 on interchange fees for payment transactions carried out on the basis of the MFI card, i.e. multilateral interchange fees, aims to ensure that the provisions of the Regulation are enforced by operators of payment schemes subject to its regime by the appointment as supervisory authority of the President of the National Bank of Poland. In turn, the competent supervisory authority for the activities of payment service providers, i.e. the publishers of payment instruments and clearing agents, will be the Financial Supervision Commission, in accordance with the draft law. The consequence is the division of supervisory functions in order to ensure the enforcement of the provisions of the MFI Regulation between the President of the NBP and the Financial Supervision Commission in such a way that the President of the NBP will exercise systemic supervision over the financial market infrastructure creating the payment system and the KNF will be the prudential supervisor of payment service providers, i.e. over clearing agents and issuers of payment instruments. The imposition on the NBP President of system surveillance duties is a consequence of the dynamic situation in the payment services market related to technological progress, in particular the development of mobile applications. Therefore, it is necessary to increase the market regulators' participation in the creation of safe and efficient schemes in which payment instruments operate. The central bank's involvement in the supervision of this payment system will build public confidence in the national currency and develop efficient payment systems for industry 4.0. Further regulations setting cybersecurity standards must follow these solutions. Digital technologies are the driving force and core of the fourth industrial revolution, in which, alongside the in-depth automation of industrial processes and global data processing, there is a far-reaching reconstruction of the functioning of modern societies. Cybersecurity is a necessary condition for reindustrialisation of the country within the framework of the Responsible Development Strategy. Creating safeguards for IT industrial and institutional applications can potentially become a Polish national specialisation due to the great intellectual potential of Polish engineering staff in this area. The Free and Solidarni Circle appreciates the government's legislative initiative and the substantive contribution of the committee's work to proceding draft laws on amending the Payment Services Act and certain other laws, and thus supports the proposed regulations, indicating the need to prepare comprehensive legal solutions in the area of cybersecurity. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u495	Declaration on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the draft law amending the Payment Services Act and certain other laws. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present a position on the draft law adapting national rules on payment transactions using payment cards to the provisions of Regulation 2015/751 of the European Parliament and of the Council on interchange fees for card-based payment transactions called the MFI Regulation. We welcome the fact that started work on adapting the Polish legal order to the resolution of this regulation, initiated by the previous government of PO-PSL found their finale in the Sejm and that Poles will have free access to the basic payment account. It is a pro-consumer solution, in addition contributing to the growth of the so-called. banking in Poland, which promotes transparency of financial flows and serves both economic turnover and the budget well. The proposed law introduces rules that provide consumers with access to a basic payment account and basic payment services linked to that account. Since many consumers believe that the payment account is only an unnecessary expense, often combined with complex payment services for which additional charges have to be paid, it is appropriate for Poland to use the national option in which the basic account access service - within certain limits of the transaction - is free of charge. This service will be provided to consumers who do not have another payment account. The consumer will only be able to execute basic payment transactions, such as deposits and withdrawals, transfer orders or direct debits. The services associated with such an account will be basic in nature and will be intended for those who do not use the bank account (the SKOK account), with the proposal to introduce a monthly limit on free transactions carried out by means of a transfer order, including fixed orders. Above this limit, the payment service provider will be able to charge the consumer a fee for the execution of subsequent credit transfer transactions, including fixed orders. However, this fee may not be higher than the most frequently charged fee for the same service linked to any other payment account maintained by the supplier. The limit for free credit transfer transactions has been set in such a way that home financial management can be managed e.g. as regards the regulation of basic obligations relating to the operation of the household (activities, electricity or telecommunications services). A consumer holding a basic account will be able to trade payment cards with the exception of credit card transactions, since no credit products will be linked to the basic account, which is the result of the decision not to use the national option - Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8 Directives. In addition, it is proposed to allow providers to charge a fee for issuing a payment card, no higher than the usual fee charged by the supplier for issuing an equivalent payment card.

Draft Print Act No. 927 contains structured records of government prints No. 626 and 785. The draft submitted today is the implementation of Union law contained in Directives 2015/751/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 29 April 2015 on interchange fees for card-based payment transactions and 2014/92/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 July 2014 on the comparability of payment accounts charges, transfer of payment accounts and access to the basic payment account. In general, the proposal harmonises the rules on payment services in EU countries. It regulates in detail the provisions relating to the operation of basic accounts, fees and control over the payment services provided. The Committee on Public Finance has adopted several amendments to clarify non-cash transactions and payment cards. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u512	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Rail Transport Act and some other laws, print No. 840. The project concerns, among others: issues such as changes in the definition of railway infrastructure components, allocation process, capacity and charging for the use of railway infrastructure, strengthening the competence and independence of the regulatory authority, the separation of service facilities and the process of making them available. The general trend of the amendment, which aims to align national definitions with European standards, should be welcomed, as is the case when the definition of infrastructure is aligned with that of Directive 2012/34/EU. It is therefore useful to refer to the introduction of a reference to the new Annex 1 to the Act, which sets out the components of the railway infrastructure in a way that is even more structured than the Directive itself. In this way, the mutual definition of railway infrastructure through the railway area and the railway area through the railway infrastructure has been eliminated, leaving only the definition of the railway area by the railway infrastructure. The proposed amendments should be accepted positively, including: in the definition of the infrastructure manager, the railway line, the railway sidewalk and the railway network. They introduce a legislative order and contribute to a better understanding of the legislation by its addressees. In addition, the introduction into the law of provisions clarifying the scope of certain provisions for infrastructure used exclusively for its own use, where the concept of private infrastructure has been introduced, and for railway infrastructure located in the repair workshops of rolling stock, wagon or locomotive plants contributes to greater flexibility in the application of the law and will certainly have a positive impact on the economics of transport companies. Similarly, a change in the scope of the exemption from the provisions of the Law in respect of railway sidings, which extends the scope of application by Chapter 6 in connection with the obligation to make railway sidings accessible to service facilities, will benefit the real economic processes. At the same time, attention should be paid to the extremely important aspect of expanding the area of automation of rail network management. The Responsible Development Strategy sets the main role for digital technologies in the so-called. the fourth industrial revolution, in which, alongside the in-depth automation of industrial processes and global data processing, there is a far-reaching reconstruction of the entire logistics infrastructure. Ensuring a high level of cybersecurity is a prerequisite for reindustrialisation of the country, including the modernisation of rail transport. The Free and Solidarnia Circle, taking into account the report of the Emergency Sub-Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Rail Transport, which sets out the uniform text of the Act, advocates the proposed project, while at the same time signalling the need to prepare comprehensive legal solutions for ensuring cybersecurity in the rail transport sector, which mark the innovative industry 4.0. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u524	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Uh-huh. Fr. Jerzy Popiełuszko is a saint who comes from my Sokółski district, I knew him and I know the family very well Fr. George. Today, 19 October 2016, we celebrate the 32nd anniversary of the martyr's death of Blessed Virgin Mary. Fr. George Popieluszko. Fr. Jerzy Popiełuszko was born on 14 September 1947 in the village Okopy near Suchowola in Podlaskie Voivodeship. He graduated from the high school in Suchowola, currently bearing his name. He started his studies at the Higher Seminary in Warsaw. In 1966, 1968 he served as a military force in Bartoszyce. In May 1972 he received priestly ordination at the hands of cardinals. Stefan Wyszyński and worked in parishes: Teeth, Anin, Warsaw. He organized religious-formative-practical meetings for medical students, hospital nurses and doctors. In August 1980, by order of the Cardinal. Stefan Wyszynski worked among the workers and the steel industry. From January 1982 he celebrated Mass on the last Sunday of the month. in the intention of the country with the participation of the crowds of believers from different parts of Poland. In homilys he preached Christian ideals of social justice, freedom, truth, love, defending fundamental human rights and human dignity as a child of God. His words were decisive and courageous. He was the herald of the Gospel principle: conquer evil with good. He actively participated in the trials of those convicted of strikes, supported their families and organized charity aid. In July 1984, he was accused by the Military Prosecutor's Office in Warsaw of defaming state power. There was a campaign of accusations and attacks against Fr. George. On October 19, 1984, when returning from pastoral service in Bydgoszcz, he was banditly assaulted and kidnapped, brutally tortured and murdered by three security officers from the Ministry of Interior, and thrown into the Vistula near Włocławek. On November 3, 1984, the funeral of the murdered priest took place. The Chaplain's body of Solidarity rested at the church of St. St. Stanisława Kostka in Warsaw. The grave of Fr. George became the new national sanctuary. On 6 June 2010, he was beatified by Pope Benedict XVI. Suchowola particularly honors his memory and his parents, Marianna and Władysław, who rest on the parish cemetery in Suchowola. A summary of the 3rd open poetry competition will be held on 23 October 2016. B. Fr. Jerzy, where poets from all over the country participate. The aim of the competition is to show the beauty and the most important truths about human relations, based on teaching of the Blessed Virgin Mary. Fr. George Popieluszko. On that day, the NSZZ delegations from all over the country will pay tribute to the blessed parents. Entering into ideas proclaimed by Fr. Jerzy, a high school from Suchowola, together with the municipality of Suchowola, carries out a project of European Peace Bread - every year the bread of peace is baked from grains originating in 12 countries of Central and Eastern Europe. The main objective of the project is to promote world peace and to promote peace through sustainable development of agriculture in Europe. Moreover, from the sale of these unique breads, scholarships were funded for gifted young people from the school in Suchowola, which bears his name. Initiatives are implemented in cooperation with the Foundation. Fr. Jerzy Popieluszko, who is led by the blessed family. Thus, Suchowola and Podlaskie repay their debt to the work, which Fr. George made it. Let him rest in peace. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u528	Statement on the 395th anniversary of the Battle of Chocimi. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! October 9th this year It has been exactly 395 years since the signing of the peace treaty ending the battle of Chocim fought between the army of the Republic of John Charles Chodkiewicz and the Turkish army under the command of Sultan Osman II. It is worth recalling this event because the battle ended with a tactical victory of the Republic of Poland. The number of forces has not yet been clearly defined. However, it is known that the Turkish army was much (two - or three times more) than Polish. And so: Polish troops were probably 25 and 26 thousand, but they received support from the Kozaks in the form of 13 regiments with a total of 22 troops. The Turkish army of Osman II had 70.75 thousand. People. She was supported by about 20,000. The Tatars and 12,000. Moldovans and Volochians. In total, the sultan could have had more than 100,000 at his disposal. soldiers, not counting tens of thousands of domestics. Polish-Lithuanian-Cossack forces were much smaller. That's why the Sultan was sure of his winnings. The Polish-Lithuanian-Cossack forces locked in the fortified camp and stood in the way of the Ottoman army near Chocim. The first battle took place near Prutu sources, where the Tatars attacked several hundred people's Cossack driveway. After a long fight, the Cossacks were defeated, and those remaining alive were murdered by the Sultan order. The news of this, transmitted by the captain, made a great impression, and the Cossacks of Prut were compared to the Spartans of Thermopil. The whole battle lasted six weeks, bloody and exhausting. The Polish-Lithuanian infantry supported by the Zaporist infantry played a major role in defending Chocim. The Cossacks took on their shoulders the main burden of fighting. They fought back five out of ten Turkish assaults. On September 24, the captain Jan Chodkiewicz died. Just before he died, he gave it to Lubomirski. Despite the loss of the chief chief, however, the will to fight did not weaken, and in the Sultan's surroundings the peace party gained the advantage. Osman II himself had had enough of the defeated battles, and the winter was approaching, and before the Turkish army there was a vision of marching through the snow-covered roadless Balkans. The peace negotiations lasted a few days. Finally, an honorable peace treaty was signed for both sides on 9 October 1621. It confirmed the findings of the Bush Treaty four years ago. According to the provisions Poland recognised Turkish authority over Moldova, Poles committed to stop the Kozaks from invaded Turkey and the Turks stopped the Tatars from invaded Poland. Dniestr remained the border of the Republic of Poland. The losses in this battle were about 8,000 on one side. Poles and 6.5 thousand. Cossacks, and on the other side, about 40,000. Turks, Volochs and Moldovans and 2,000. Tatars. The Battle of Chocim was not only the biggest defence operation in the history of Poland, but also the largest positional war in Europe at that time. The overall result was undoubtedly the success of the Polish-Cossack troops. Chocimia's defense confirmed the validity of the old-Polish martial arts saying that the rhyme, musket and kicking victory are cooking. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u532	Declaration on the 110th anniversary of granting city rights to Puławy. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 2016 is the year of celebration of the 110th anniversary of granting city rights to Puławy. Puławy is a city in the western part of the Lublin Voivodeship located near the border of the Mazowieckie Voivodeship, located on the Vistula River, on the edge of the Małopolska Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula Vistula. It is an industrial centre: chemical, construction, pharmaceutical, scientific industry: five scientific and research institutes, higher and tourist and cultural education which is part of the tourist triangle Puławy - Kazimierz Dolny - Nałęczów. The first quotations of the village lying in the place of Puławy date back to the end of the 15th century. Since the second half of the 17th century. Puławy was owned by Lubomirskis who built their summer residence here. The period of greatness of Puławy, which lasted for 100 years, began in 1731, when Puławy became the property of the Czartoryski family. The remains of their existence are a beautiful palace-park complex located near the cliff of the Vistula. Puławy city rights were granted in 1906, 12 years before Poland regained its independence. The decision to make New Alexandria, Mokradki and Zarobne Puławy a city was made by the tsarist administration, which has controlled these lands since the partitions. It was a gesture aimed at trying to strengthen the power of the Russian possessor by developing a local tsar administration powered by local residents. Previous failed attempts to grant urban rights to Puławy took place in 1824 and 1840. Postwar history of the city dominated the decision to build nitrogen plants by the Polish authorities in 1960. The plants started production in 1966. After that date there was a significant increase in the population and the character of the village changed. From the small town of Puławy they became an industrial city. The Jubilee Year in Puławy abounds with many events commemorating this beautiful anniversary. The programme of the event, which has already started in spring this year, includes, among others: concerts, recitals, festivals, competitions, exhibitions, fun and sports events. An important event in the framework of this event was the solemn session of the city council on the occasion of the 110th anniversary of Puławy, which took place on 2 October 2016 in the Polish Chamber of Chemistry. Before the session, the invited guests gathered at the Holy Mass at the church of St. Our Lady of the Rosary. Among the invited guests who celebrated their presence were: Princess Barbara Czartoryska, head of the Presidential Office of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda Małgorzata Sadurska, MPs at the Polish Parliament Krzysztof Szulowski and Jakub Kulesza, representative of the Lublin Voivodeship, honorary citizens of Puławy, persons awarded with badges for their service to Puławy. There were also councillors of Puławy City Council, local government officials, clergymen, residents of Puławy and many excellent guests. The ceremonial session was started by a lecture by Dr. Robert Derewenda in the history of the nation and the Polish state, from which the gathered could learn about the significant influence of our city and people associated with it on the turbulent history of Poland. After the historical presentation, a screening of the film "Modern Puławy" was held showing what the city looks like 110 years after the granting of city rights. Then four people: Halina Solecka, Zbigniew Mark Śliwiński, Jan Stefan Wawr and Jerzy Witashko were awarded Honorary Badges for their service to Puławy. Many invited guests spoke. The President of Puławy and the President of Puławy City Council also received congratulations letters. The ceremonial session was celebrated by the performances of Puławy artists: the band "Small elevens" from Primary School No. 11, the Song and Dance Team "Powiśle," the ballet band "Etiuda" and dance studios "Pokus" and several Puławy vocalists. For all those who have contributed to the development of the city and its beautiful current shape over the years, I offer my heartfelt thanks and congratulations. Today, I wish the people who are in charge of the city's enormous potential and future. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-19-sejm-28-1.u533	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On October 18th, we celebrated Polish Post Day. The date of birth of the post office in Poland is 18 October 1558. It was then that King Zygmunt Augustus established a permanent postal link between Kraków and Venice through Vienna, by means of mail, that is, space horses. The public institution set up by the monarch was primarily intended to maintain permanent diplomatic and economic contacts with other European countries. The king also wanted to protect himself from the control of the imperial post office, which so far carried Polish letters to Italy. The decision to create a post has accelerated the King's interests. A year after Bony Zygmunt August died, he started a lawsuit for his mother's inheritance. Bona, from the Sforza family, decided to leave Poland, exporting wealth accumulated for more than 40 years. Recovering wealth, money from debtors and obtaining confirmation of the rights of Zygmunt Augustus to Italian succession involved the activity of Polish diplomacy and many advisors and agents. Maintaining constant communication between them and the Polish king required a regular postal connection. On the occasion of such a special holiday, I wish all communicationsmen, posters and employees of Polish Post to wish health and success in professional and private life. At the same time, I wish that your important social service is a source of personal satisfaction and an example of responsibility, loving assistance and sacrifice for the welfare of another person.
